He just wanted to talk to her about how good he thought her work was, and how she should be careful, because there were a lot of creeps out there, and she was vulnerable, wasn’t she? Making films like that!


Or maybe she was actually wacko? Oh my god! He hadn’t even thought of that. How she might have been out to get him the whole time? A fucking vampire! Seducing him the way she had. Like maybe she and Yuna had planned this all along? It was punishing. They were all so punishing. Why was he being punished?


No!—the video had been made before he’d ever met Yuna. So, no. Or had it? Maybe not. How was he supposed to know what was real? That snake tattoo. It was otherworldly. It wasn’t sane, the way it seemed to make him lose control. Venomous or vampiric? He couldn’t tell.


He was losing control. He was losing fucking control.


God, he needed Sean. He needed Sean to help him sort this out. Sean would see right through it all. He would know how to tell the figments and freaks in the fairy tale apart from those of true heart.


Get a fucking grip, man!


And then it slithered into his mind. The video.


But it wasn’t Troy/Masha getting fucked by a handful of gods. Now it was him, Trevor, and the gods were Sarah, Ariadne, Masha, and Yuna, and they were all there 
watching him down on the ground, looking at him from above. He could hear another voice too, like a swan hissing. No, fuck! It was the opera music. They were all singing.


He had a rock in his hand.


They were naked, all of them, wraiths with white robes falling off them like shed snakeskin, and their vulvas were swirling, morphing into pulsing phalluses. They were coming for him. There was nothing he could do. All along they had been coming for him. They were going to gangbang him, spit their venom all over his face.


Finally, finally drown him.




———


He felt himself sink into poison. Then silence.


He lay on the ground, spent and sticky.


And then panic started to build.




———



				It came as though from behind a wall, a small voice. 
“Hey, you fucking loser.”


And again, 
“Hey, asshole.”


“Shut the fuck up, man.”


“Asshole. You fucking piece of shit, you fucking loser. Psychotic fucking loser, afraid of a little pussy? Afraid the pussies are going to get you?”


“Get a fucking grip, Trevor! You’re going wacko! Breathe, Trevor. Breathe. Stop for a second. Just one second, shut up!”


He breathed. And breathed again. Every time a word started, he breathed. And his asshole relaxed. And the panic settled a bit, and with the panic settling, the space between 
words grew, and the internal constriction, too. He was in a lot of pain. He would have to get X-rays, and painkillers. A splint of some kind, probably.


Later.


First, Trevor had to finish reading.


Read through it all. Get a sense of what he was up against with Sarah. Then, see to his foot.


After that, call Sarah.


He still had time. He could catch her in her own game.




———


Or maybe they had to figure this fucking thing out? Instead of playing whatever insane game they’d been playing, with all these moves going on behind his back he hadn’t even seen.




———


He could feel the wraiths pulling at his mind, the ogre feeding him images, urging him to look, to turn away from here. What keywords had he used to first conjure the video? He needed to find it, watch it again, here, now! To see if there were any clues he’d missed. Release the tension! It could give him a clear mind. A clear mind. Show him what to do.




———


No!


He closed the cinematic eye. He wasn’t going to look. Not right now.


He was going to keep reading.




———


Trevor pulled the keyboard and mouse pad down and perched them on the splintered chair, holding his leg with the broken toes out to the side, his other leg curled in, footpad resting on his thigh, almost comforting. It was awkward angling his head up, but Sarah liked a big screen, so he could still read easily enough after magnifying the text.


He felt humble down there on the floor, on top of Sarah’s flower-print rug. A relief being unseated.


He gave himself an assignment: read everything there was to read. He was going to concentrate on his work, piece things together to figure out what to do next. He needed the information first.


In one of the emails from Sarah to Ariadne, he read:


I’ve attached the first instalment of your memoir based on your notes—I’m calling it
 A Song: Mummification—
along with a story I’ve written, “Merriment.” I’m so excited, Ariadne! For the way words are forming into stories. It’s hard to explain. Stories have always been Trevor’s thing, not mine. They used to be, but it’s been so long since I felt compelled to write one. For a long time there were the articles, and then blogging became the work I needed to be doing. Knowing how isolated I felt, knowing it must be like this for other moms, too. How hard it was so often, every single day for all those years. Total over whelm intermixed with all this love. Always feeling guilty for needing so much more, feeling alone in it all. Wanting to get it right. Trying to be there for Trevor but not feeling like he understood, or cared enough to be there for me.


Then I realized that no matter how much love I gave to Trevor, it would never be enough for us both.


“Merriment” is about a story I believed in for a long time. And the spectacle of the mystical wife. It makes me laugh and cry at the same time. I’m sending it along—be gentle when you read! Tradesies—I’ll write your story if you’ll read mine!


“Merriment”? Hadn’t he seen that title recently? Something one of his students had written?


It was familiar, but he couldn’t place it. Still, there was a feeling he’d seen it somewhere. Noticed but irrelevant, so not fully taken in.


Trevor double-clicked on Sarah’s story.




———



				Afterward, astonished by the power in Sarah’s voice, he felt himself morph between his and Sarah’s variations—wife, ogre, man. Somewhere in the morphing, for a brief moment, he felt another presence—a terrified little boy, hiding, shivering uncontrollably, crouched in a little ball, hands over his ears, saying, 
“No, no, no, no, no, no, no,”
 and then hitting his ears, 
“Be quiet, Trevor, not now, Trevor.”
 Screaming and screaming at the nightmare it all was.


No one came.


Was he a monster? Or was he a man, breathing in a room he had once shared with a woman? How could he live with himself as both?


How can I trust Trevor to positively influence my daughters, 
given what he makes of women? Given how he tortures himself in his own mind?
 Sarah had written this in one of her emails to Ariadne. “Positively influence” his daughters? She expected this of him? What did she even mean by it? They were his 
daughters
, not women. Why was Sarah confusing these things in her mind? Positive influence was her schtick. What did he have to contribute to that, anyway? He was always there for shits and giggles. To distract when Sarah was too busy with other things, until she was ready to take over again. He towed her fucking line with all the running around, and it was never enough anyway!




———


Sarah’s stories—these variations on his own that painted him in such different light.


How hard he worked to be a man. What did they expect of him?


He was terrified!


Some part of him had always been terrified. That pathetic little boy, cowering inside him. Why did they all hate him?




———



				The boy was cowering from the ogre and the man, trying to scramble behind Bukowski for some reason, as though Bukowski could understand. But all Bukowski wanted to do was give the boy a drink and fuck his mom. They were all there but not there. Pretending the little boy did not exist, but he did! And they could feel him. And Sarah could feel him. She was always trying to feel through the man 
and the ogre to 
him
. Through the failure and rage when he did not get what he wanted, which infuriated him!


The ogre wanted to eat the boy. The man wished to ignore him until he went away, or could be forgotten altogether, so that the man could turn his attention to other things, pretending he could not hear him whimper. Bukowski was drunk and eloquent, gifting words that entertained the others, making them all feel that perhaps the boy had finally gone away. Almost as though the pitiful creature did not exist. But Sarah saw him. And she wanted Trevor to see him. But why should Trevor have to? If Sarah had been taking care of him properly, like she was supposed to! It was her job, not his!


The ogre had a mind to destroy it all! Scatter the debris in the gaping worthless emptiness.




———


Trevor looked at the boy. The boy was holding something. A rock? No. It was something dark blue, and glowing. A beautiful orb.


Trevor reached. He reached toward the boy, wanting to help the boy bring the orb up to the light so the others might see it too. But the boy recoiled, knowing Trevor could not be trusted.




———



				There was one last folder to open. 
Letter.
 Trevor did not want to open it. He knew enough. More than enough. He did not need to know more. Yet he had set himself the task. 
If he left this last folder out, he might miss key pieces that Sarah would hold against him.


There was one file inside. A scanned copy of a handwritten letter. Trevor froze when he saw Sarah’s name spelled out in Yuna’s unmistakable calligraphy.


Dear Sarah,


There are certain instances of skin that bind together people who have never touched.


We have shared vibration through your husband’s flesh.


I believed in him because my skin glowed—


like a gathering of stars


scattering dust to earth, long ago.


Cohering to form a new layer atop my body.


I transgressed an invisible line—loving him, knowing he was bound to you.


Choosing to believe you accepted this love because I trusted him.


Lost in his illusion.


Unseeing that which I did not wish to see—that love shared under illusion is duplicitous.


I did not keep to my orbit, crashing against him. Allowing his force to destabilize me.




———


After the explosion, I learned there was no surface, just a vacuum of ongoing space.


I passed through the space, but it disappeared me. For a time, I was annihilated. He was there at the ready, capturing the light.




———


Life regenerates in the aftermath of cosmic collision.




———


My skin pulses. Wanting to shed the remnants of the illusion bound up in words that still hold power over me, after all this time.


“I trust you to never tell.” “My wife and I have an implicit understanding.” “The world does not understand love like ours.”




———


He no longer holds power over my words.


Hurt now lives where the words come from—yet to heal requires honesty.


I haven’t been the only besotted underling, Sarah.


Is it wrong of me to use your name? I don’t want to reduce you, to disappear you as though my actions do not create an impact on your life.


I played my part.


I do not wish to harm in retribution, but I do feel a need to take responsibility, to disrupt the pattern so he does not go on taking advantage of others, as he did me. As he has others. Perhaps you allow it, or perhaps you don’t know. I cannot know the truth based on his dishonest words.


I feel your skin upon my skin—a presence binding us. I reach through its form toward you, offering you my truth, knowing you may not wish to see it, knowing too that abuses perpetuate if we choose to look away from the pain.


Yuna Tanaka


The truth was unendurable.


Trevor hobbled to the bathroom and grabbed the bottle of T3s from the medicine cabinet, with a mind to swallow them all. But the bottle was almost empty. Only three left. He put them on his tongue, tasting the acrid medicine, put his mouth under the tap and swallowed hard, then hobbled back to the bed, nestling himself in the covers, mind emptied out. He lay, unmoving, ferried to codeine dreams once the painkillers kicked in, floating close to the source where life and death intermixed and blurred between one another, washed in blue twilight.

OceanofPDF.com















Inside the ogre’s hope chest were garments and gold and Trevor’s grandfather’s pocket watch. Bones of small animals and pretty brassieres. Hockey cards and sticks, rotting leaves, books, and books, and torn out pages of books, as well as student assignments. It could hold the world; it went on forever. The dreamer pulled out a paper, and on it was a poem:


Light Source


A tree aglow


kindness


burned into kindness.


There were snapshots of memories too: Sarah saying “Tell me your dreams.” Sarah, wearing an apron in the kitchen, spatchcocking a chicken. Sarah laughing in front of Rosenau Palace, in love. With him, or a him she believed him to be. He could feel her love and he wanted it to never end.


A pair of black swans.


How did the ogre get Trevor’s good memories and hide them away in here?


Him inside Yuna in the tree, Yuna inside him in Yuna’s bedroom.


Masha reading from her essay in class.


Arlynn kissing all over his face.


Amelie holding his hand.


Ramona submitting her thesis, the look on her face telling him she knew she had succeeded, sharing this with him.


Ariadne reading a poem in a dark pub, smoking a clove cigarette outside, the smell, the longing, the smoke.


Worn Bukowski paperbacks, heavily annotated by a young Trevor.


Sean plunging into the ocean, holding finishing nails in his lips in his living room, cracking open a beer. Looking at Trevor with eyes that did not make Trevor want to hide himself away, as the eyes of most men did.


His sister Mimi laughing at the pond, him chasing her—both of them with sticks in their mouths, slapping their feet like beaver tails.


His bike and an empty mind, flashes of the world as he breathed, up to the top of the hill, and then, water as far as he could see.


Sarah handing him the smallest book of empty pages, inviting him to write the story with her.


Underneath all the flotsam, a trunk-bottom full of marbles, holding light.




———


The ogre had taken all this precious life and hidden it away, all these moments that felt like love when Trevor looked at them. These sacred gifts, hoarding them.


Making it so he often forgot they were there. He felt the ogre’s hand reach inside his abdomen, where the chest was hidden away, and slam the lid down.


“You don’t deserve my treasures.”


“But they’re mine.”




———


The ogre opened another box.


And in it were Trevor’s failures that had somehow been turned into corruptions instead of mistakes that could have been repaired. Abominations.


A plagiarized poem. A fake dream.
 Dream the Text Like a Persistent Certainty.
 A dungeon in a Transylvanian castle, bruises on Sarah’s ass and thighs after hitting too hard. An email that read, “It never happened.”


All the times he saw but looked away, because he could, because he knew if he did, someone else would deal with it. Everything he heard but pretended not to hear if it worked to his advantage.


Sarah, sleepless for a hundred years. No prince come to relieve her from having to hold it all together so that she could fall asleep and dream. Locking her in a dungeon to do love’s dirty work out of sight, out of mind—a baby at each of her breasts and her womb leaking miscarriages. Blood and milk and blood. Still with a smile on her face and a little cheer for Trevor to keep doing his best, because she believed in him.


Yuna in a tree, weeping.


The empty book of a hundred pages, still blank, never a word written in. Though he’d meant to, he’d meant to. Someday he would.


Smart pretty girls wanting his attention, and him giving it to them, asking too much in return. Giving them love that damaged and confused, rather than knowledge that would set them alight for the parts of their lives that would come after. Giving that was taking, always taking, feeding and never 
thinking about the hunger of others after his fill. Only his own hunger. Only his own fill—everything in his sight, his to take if he was able.


“All I wanted was love,” said the little boy, turning a blue marble in the light, watching it stretch across time—disfigured and beautiful.


“And when you received it,” grunted the ogre, “what did you give in return?”


“I was just a boy,” said the boy.


“And then you weren’t any longer,” the ogre sniggered back.


“But I still am,” protested the man.


“Are you?” asked the boy. “Who is taking care of me, then?”


“I am,” said the ogre, grabbing the boy and putting him in the chest with all the beautiful memories.




———


Trevor fell asleep in his bed and woke up with his right hand to his neck, his eyes landing on the photograph of him and Sarah on their honeymoon in front of Rosenau Palace—those two beautiful, pitiful black swans.


He threw his pillow at the framed photograph, missing, knocking their wedding photo off the wall behind the dresser. He heard the unmistakable sound of shattering glass, feeling guilt, and satisfaction, and movement in something that had been stuck for a good long time.


He stood at the doorway, bracing himself.


Looking back across the threshold he saw the slightly darker rectangle on the wall. A spectre of empty space. The 
broken glass was there behind the dresser, he knew. He had heard it smash.


If he walked away, left it there, no one would come to clean it up.


There might be shards embedded in the rug and he might cut his already mangled foot—or if he didn’t deal with it now, he might forget, and then when the girls were back, one of them might crawl under the dresser.


Pain shot up from his toes to his gut when he tried to pull the dresser away from the wall, so he got down on his knees and looked underneath. The glass was shattered into large shards, most of which were still in the frame. He angled the frame, sliding it along the floor, and when he pulled it up, trying to keep what he could intact, one of the shards slipped, cutting his pointer finger and thumb to the bone. Blood pooled to the surface of his skin, fast and hot, seeping through the cracks in the glass, all over Sarah’s white dress, staining it red.




———


The story stopped.




———


Time stood still for a hundred years.




———


Starting up again when he heard a familiar voice shouting from downstairs.


His heart crashing wildly inside his chest.


It couldn’t be.


It couldn’t be.


He had to still be dreaming.


“Upstairs!” Trevor shouted back, just in case it was real. “I’m all fucked up. I can’t come down.”


He heard the unmistakable footsteps.




———


The bedroom door opened, and Sean stood there looking down at the floor, observing the mess Trevor had made.


“Hart? What the fuck, man? Are you okay?”


“Sean! Are you really here? Or am I hallucinating? I must be hallucinating. What the fuck? Are you really here? Where have you been? Everyone thought you were dead.”


“No, not dead, man, just had to disappear out of your dissociated fantasy for a while. Looks like you might need some help. I’ll get you to the hospital and we can tell each other our stories along the way.”




OceanofPDF.com















I’m looking for you and looking for you and looking for you. Where are you hiding my children? I’m digging beneath rocks and making holes with my badger claws. My teeth are points. My claws are daggers. I can dig a sett deeper than the winding length of your intestines, deep, deep, deep inside the earth. All of me is muscle and fur. All of me is hunger. All of me is love and you are between me and my children. I am looking for them but you are trying to fill up the dream, distract me. Playing a game I do not wish to play.


I would rip out your intestines. I would gnaw them to shreds, disembowel you. You try to keep me playing. Believing I mean you harm, which I never have. Though I sometimes daydreamed revenge when the harm you cast upon me felt like too much to bear.




———


And then I find them, small children badgers with gorgeous silver fur, streaked black and white, claws black and thick as pencils, sharp points they stroke my nipples with when they suckle, scratching my itchy skin. I find them colouring in books of humans dressed as badgers doing badgerlike human things. The images in the books are streaked with soil from my children badgers’ dirty paws. They are gleeful at the saturated colours of their markers, which you must have gifted them, being a human man. I see you on a rock outcropping, well out of reach, looking down at us as though from a great distance. You are wearing thick gauntlet gloves with steel mesh, so as not to be bitten. You are armoured, as though a medieval knight. But I can see the 
rust streaks and know you are masking your vulnerability with old metal. That you do not trust the strength of it. That you have forgotten to tend to your armour, and that it has therefore become weak. Still, I have no interest in biting you now that I have found my children. I spit the taste of you out. You think you know my ferocity, fear that it will drive itself into you. But I am only here to collect my children and return to the earth, knowing the costs of your service.


I click to them. They come.


We return to the earth and leave you with the rusty metal that does not protect your heart, or allow you to share it. It seeps into you. Your heart clanks its loneliness. We hear the tinny sound. The badger children have forgotten the sound of a human heart and do not register your clanking. I look back at you, human eye memories flitting behind the screen of my badger seeing. A feeling rises in my throat, something akin to kindness.


I wish for you to learn how to take off your armour without fear. To be creaturely. The children flit between badger and human form. They are of both of us. I fear they will forget they are always both badger and human, that you will trick them into the human world. Or if not you, then the world that tricked you into forgetting yourself, arming you with metal skin in which you have forgotten how to do anything but rust in place, feeling yourself to be a victim of falling rain. “Take your costume off!” I try to shout, but what comes out is,“GRRkkkaaallff csst uff.”




———


Sarah fell asleep in Ariadne’s guest room and woke up to see the moon floating in a starlit sky, its light stirring her. How long since she’d dreamt badger dreams! She closed her eyes, falling back under, awake to it all.




OceanofPDF.com















I went to sleep at the river in a strange costume and awoke in my own skin. The river took me. The river flowed outside and inside, and through. I swallowed its waters. Its waters were life, unbidden. It entered where it could enter without harming me. Time cycled in the dream, months of ablution in the melted surge of millennia-old ice. Minuscule creatures of epic proportions and ancient memories suspended within.


It is not exactly right to talk of cleansing, because I was not dirty. But my body did not know its own truth.


The river remained unknowable.


I became again. Unwritten. A thing never made word.


I no longer felt I had a part to play.


All my possible selves coalesced.


In the dream I stripped the gaze embossed in my flesh, and my humanity went with it.


It is dangerous to be naked, we are taught to forget how.


If you see me, walking clothed in the world, you will never know.


My sovereignty is total, and nebulous.




———


Masha fell asleep entwined in Yuna’s arms and woke up feeling alive, wanting to share her dream.




OceanofPDF.com















Clouds danced on the mountain


we had known that as myth.


Yuna opened their eyes and saw Masha looking at them, lines from their dream lingering. They turned over, wrote them down quickly, then turned back to Masha, whispering, “Tell me your dreams?”

Thanks to the living processes for decomposition, reorientations, growth, and ongoing learning.


Thanks to those who come closest to light up and practise dying with one another so we might learn what we are.


Thank you to my parents for loving in the ways they knew how and giving what they had to give and more, because parenthood asks more than any of us are prepared for.


Thank you to my sisters, who dreamt and continue to dream alongside me.


Thank you to my children, who offer me the gift of their becoming and unconditional love, who challenge me daily to live up to better ways of honouring what we are given. Most parts of most days I dance in gratitude to have 
been blessed with the calling. The madness works itself out in dreams.


Thank you to the writers and thinkers whose works have shaped my mind. There’s no way to name all the varied influences in this sensational, astounding, confusing, emergent, complexly intertwined world. I thank the named, and those I haven’t named all the same.


Thank you to my neighbours, for reciprocal care and nourishment.


Thank you to Carolyn Himmelright, for keeping the house I have made home affordable to live some kind of writing life in. Thank you to the house and everything it holds.


Hami’yaa to the land, waters, and beings I have been privileged to learn love among over many years and seasons on the unceded territories of the Gitxsan people, who have cultivated kinship among land, rivers, and more-than-human beings since time immemorial.
